# New UNREVIEWED fixed-reference fiber constancy and conditional EP route
Binding: 0c2f95971b70de6a29474c53553314a7263adae73a415392785673779713019e

# Fixed curve reference measures and fiber constancy

This is an unreviewed reconstruction for original P16.1 and the shared singular-quotient obstruction in P13.2. It does not assert target-side genuine psh membership, singular EP, or a solved original. The independent native Runge continuation83 has frozen input and receives no feedback from this draft.

## 1. The precise fiber claim

Let \(k\) be algebraically closed, trivially valued, of characteristic zero and of any cardinality. Let \(\pi:Y\to X\) be projective birational, with \(Y\) smooth projective integral, \(X\) projective integral geometrically unibranch, and \(L\) an actual ample rational line bundle on \(X\). Put \(\theta=\pi^*c_1(L)\). The fibers are geometrically connected: factor through the finite universal-homeomorphism normalization and apply normal-target Stein factorization to the resolution, as reconstructed with exact Stacks hypotheses in continuation81.

For an actual bounded \(\varphi\in\operatorname{PSH}(Y,\theta)\), BJ Definition4.1 gives a decreasing NET \(\varphi_i\in H_{\mathbb R}(L_i)\) with \(c_1(L_i)\to\theta\). Boundedness of the limit makes each finite-stage \(L_i\) semiample after passage to a tail, since its generating sections have no common zero. Retain the actual canonical reference metric on each \(L_i\). Do not assume that this is a sequence of sections of \(\pi^*L\): same-bundle regularization in the semiample class is exactly part of the unresolved descent issue.

The proposal is to show \(\varphi\) constant on each analytic fiber. Then apply the assertion to \(\max(\varphi,-m)\), which belongs to the same nef class, to obtain constancy for every unbounded actual \(\varphi\), including fibers with value \(-\infty\).

## 2. Fixed model background on a single proper curve

Fix an analytic point \(s\in X^{an}\), \(F=\mathscr H(s)\), and a complete algebraically closed nontrivially valued extension \(K/F\). Let \(f:C\to Y_K\) be a morphism from a smooth proper connected \(K\)-curve whose composite to \(X_K\) is the \(K\)-point defined by \(s\). Write \(\varphi_i^C=\varphi_i\circ f^{an}\).

The following geometric/reference applications M1–M3 must be established in full; they are explicit antecedents of the deduction below, rather than silently inferred from a numerical class:

* M1: choose ONE projective admissible formal model \(\mathfrak C\) of \(C\), dominating the graph closure of \(f\) into the constant formal model of \(Y_K\), and carrying the pullbacks of ALL the canonical references \(L_i\). Its map to the constant model of \(X_K\) factors through the section \(\operatorname{Spec}K^\circ\to X_{K^\circ}\) extending the constant point. This section exists by properness. There are finitely many reduced special-fiber curve components \(E\), and their associated analytic points form one fixed finite set \(\Sigma\subset I(C)\).
* M2: the curvature \(\nu_i\) of the pulled canonical reference on \(L_i|_C\) has the raw model formula \(\nu_i=\sum_{E}a_{E,i}\delta_{\zeta_E}\), where \(a_{E,i}\ge0\) and each coefficient is the fixed multiplicity/residue factor times the degree of \(L_i\) on the image of \(E\). The pulled FS metric has positive curvature \(\mu_i\), with \(dd^c\varphi_i^C=\mu_i-\nu_i\). Fundamental-cycle and residue factors remain in this formula.
* M3: for each of these fixed proper curves over the residue-field extension of \(k\), degree is a continuous linear functional on \(N^1(Y)\). Numerical triviality is preserved under extension of algebraically closed fields. Since the map of \(E\) to \(X\) is constant, \(\deg_E\theta=0\). Consequently \(a_{E,i}\to0\), and \(\|\nu_i\|\to0\) on the FIXED finite support.

The graph model is selected from the fixed morphism \(f\), not separately from each \(i\). Even though the line bundles \(L_i\) vary, they are all pulled back from the same constant model of \(Y\). This is the proposed reason that a common reference model can work. A complete proof must verify admissible-model domination, reference base-change identity and the numerical degree functional; no normalization-finiteness assertion over an arbitrary nondiscrete valuation ring is inserted.

## 3. Local Green correction with a uniform limit

Choose a connected strict affinoid neighborhood \(V\) in \(C^{an}\), with nonempty boundary, containing the tested point in its interior, and with \(\partial V\cap\Sigma=\varnothing\). For \(\zeta\in\Sigma\cap V\), Thuillier3.3.7(ii) gives a finite continuous lisse \(g_\zeta^V\), zero on \(\partial V\), with
\[
dd^c g_\zeta^V=-\delta_\zeta\quad\text{on the interior of }V.
\]
The actual primary sign is retained. Put
\[
p_i=-\sum_{\zeta\in\Sigma\cap V}a_{\zeta,i}g_\zeta^V.
\]
Then \(dd^cp_i=\nu_i\) in the interior and \(\|p_i\|_{C^0(V)}\to0\), because the support is fixed and finite and every coefficient tends to zero. Thus \(h_i=\varphi_i^C+p_i\) is continuous with positive local curvature \(\mu_i\). Wanner4.7, on the analytification of this smooth proper curve over the complete algebraically closed nontrivially valued \(K\), makes \(h_i\) Thuillier-subharmonic. No unrestricted higher-dimensional positive-current converse is invoked.

The \(h_i\) need not decrease. It is the genuine \(\varphi_i^C\) that decreases, while \(p_i\) tends to zero UNIFORMLY. Let \(D\subset V^\circ\) be any strict affinoid comparison domain, and let \(q\) be harmonic near \(D\) with \(\varphi^C\le q\) on \(\partial D\). For every \(\epsilon>0\), compactness and the decreasing continuous majorants imply that eventually \(\varphi_i^C<q+\epsilon\) on the ENTIRE boundary. This is the decreasing-superlevel finite-intersection argument, not uniform convergence to a discontinuous limit. Also eventually \(|p_i|<\epsilon\) on \(D\). Subharmonic comparison gives \(h_i\le q+2\epsilon\) on \(D\), hence \(\varphi_i^C\le q+3\epsilon\) there. Let the net decrease, then \(\epsilon\downarrow0\).

This proves \(\varphi^C\) subharmonic. It is bounded and usc on the proper connected curve, so it attains a finite maximum. Thuillier3.1.11 makes the maximum set open; usc makes it closed. Therefore \(\varphi^C\) is constant on \(C^{an}\).

Small total reference mass with MOVING unbounded Green potentials would not suffice. Fixed finite support and its finite continuous local Green functions are the indispensable extra facts.

## 4. Every original analytic point can be compared

The geometrically connected reduced projective fiber \(Y_s\) has a closed point \(z\) with residue field finite over \(F\). That extension has a unique absolute value because \(F\) is complete; it defines a fixed analytic fiber point. Fix any other analytic point \(y\) in the original fiber. Extend its completed residue field \(\mathscr H(y)\) to a complete algebraically closed nontrivially valued \(K\); if needed first adjoin a nontrivial Gauss variable. Embed the finite residue field of \(z\) into \(K\) over \(F\). Its norm is unchanged by uniqueness.

Both \(y\) and \(z\) now lift to \(K\)-rational points of the scheme fiber. The latter is geometrically connected and projective. Resolve its finitely many reduced irreducible components and use ordinary Bertini on a blowup of two smooth points to join preimages within each component. Its finite component-intersection graph gives a finite chain of morphisms from smooth proper connected curves joining the two points, as in continuation81. These are curve MORPHISMS, including resolution/blowup maps, not merely smooth embedded curves.

Apply the preceding zero-background curve assertion to every curve in this chain. Actual base-change pullback preserves the original values of the scalar functions \(\varphi_i\) and \(\varphi\). Thus \(\varphi(y)=\varphi(z)\). One comparison point \(z\) works for every \(y\); no common valued overfield for all points, density-plus-usc inference, or simultaneous descent of all potentials is required.

For unbounded \(\varphi\), the genuine floors decrease in the same nef class. Fiber constancy of every floor implies constancy of \(\varphi\) with its actual finite or \(-\infty\) value.

## 5. The exact descent limit

If M1–M3 and the indicated curve/reference identities hold, define \(\psi(s)\) to be the common fiber value. Properness makes this descended function usc: the closed superlevel set downstairs is the proper image of the corresponding closed superlevel set upstairs. It is finite at the generic trivial point if \(\varphi\) is.

This provides an actual scalar function \(\psi\) with \(\psi\circ\pi^{an}=\varphi\). It does NOT yet prove \(\psi\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,c_1(L))\). The new Runge/singular-stratum theorem and global class compatibility are needed for that first target-side membership. Smooth EP on \(Y\) alone does not establish onto pullback to the normal singular \(X\); BJ5.13 needs approaching EP on the target. Arbitrary-field finite-data obstacle descent, normalization/E1/product transport and actual finite-energy equations remain additional gates.

## 6. A concrete model application and its remaining limit

BE Proposition5.13 (LF1973–1990) supplies a useful part of M1 without imposing discrete valuation. Given a projective model of the curve and the constant projective model \(Y_{K^\circ}\), take the schematic flat closure of the graph of \(f\) in their product. It dominates the chosen curve model and extends \(f\). Pullback of EVERY line bundle on the constant target is now available on this ONE graph model. The proposition identifies the induced model metric with the actual pullback, not only its numerical class. The reference is the trivial metric over \(k\), whose constant model after base extension induces exactly that metric (BE5.9,5.10 and the local-frame definition).

The composite graph-model morphism to \(X_{K^\circ}\) agrees on the generic fiber with the extension of the constant \(K\)-point. Since the model is flat over \(K^\circ\), its generic fiber is schematically dense; separatedness of \(X_{K^\circ}\) extends the equality to the whole model. Thus every special component maps to one point of \(X_{\widetilde K}\).

BE4.20 (LF1750–1775) permits integral closure in the reduced generic fiber and proves its finiteness specifically when the complete nontrivially valued \(K\) is ALGEBRAICALLY CLOSED. This is the actual auxiliary field chosen in section4. It also gives reduced special fiber. This precise theorem does not permit arbitrary densely valued fields, as BE4.21 explicitly explains. No such general finiteness was used.

These primary statements establish the graph domination and reference identity, but the remaining application still must relate that one admissible graph model to a suitable semistable refinement and identify its curve curvature formula. Wanner3.26–3.28 give the formal metric and slope formulas, citing semistable reduction and the pertinent primary slope theorem. Every hypothesis and the domination of the chosen graph model must be verified; existence of SOME semistable model alone does not prove this refinement statement. The numerical base-extension statement in M3 also remains to be justified.

## 7. The exact conditional route from descent to EP

Suppose M1–M3, the actual curve/reference restrictions and geometric fiber chain are established. Suppose additionally that the unresolved countable-field Runge/global theorem C1 proves the following precise conclusion, with its original reference compatibility: every scalar function descended in section5 belongs to \(\operatorname{PSH}(X,L)\), for every countable algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field and actual ample rational \(L\). C1 is an explicit hypothesis here, not an accepted result of continuation83 or this draft.

Then \(\pi^*:\operatorname{PSH}(X,L)\to\operatorname{PSH}(Y,\pi^*L)\) is onto. Actual pullback gives the forward inclusion by BJ4.7. Birationality identifies the divisorial valuations of the two common function fields; BJ4.20 defines both topologies by their values on precisely this set. Pullback is therefore a homeomorphism, and surjectivity of the analytic map identifies their suprema. The normalized spaces are homeomorphic.

The upstairs class \(\pi^*c_1(L)\) is nef on smooth projective \(Y\). BJ5.20 and5.21 give compactness of its normalized genuine psh space. The homeomorphism makes the downstairs normalized space compact. BJ5.11, valid for ANY numerical class on the target, now gives its envelope property. This uses that theorem's equivalence after actual onto membership has been proved. BJ5.13 is not used to obtain that membership, because its hypotheses already require approaching target EP.

This conditional argument has no premise involving a closed invariant subset or a uniform-continuity statement on divisorial points. It uses actual all-point fiber descent and exactly the divisorial topology already defining the genuine spaces.

To remove the countable-field restriction, continuation80 sections5–6 provide a separate conditional finite-obstacle/finite-competitor route. Keep its C2–C4 as explicit operator/descent hypotheses until proved. BJ4.15 supplies decreasing continuous finite \(H^{dom}(L)\) majorants without assuming EP on a singular target. Given any competitor \(q\le f\) for a continuous PL obstacle, compactness implies that these majorants eventually satisfy \(h_i\le f+\epsilon\), by the decreasing closed-set finite-intersection argument. Only each individual finite \(h_i-\epsilon\), its model, sections and nef data must descend to an enlarged countable algebraically closed subfield. Real finite weights remain real constants. One must not assert that an arbitrary net or all competitors descend to one field.

The countable envelope pulled back from the original obstacle field has actual orthogonality on every such enlarged countable field if continuous MA base-change pushforward and the fundamental-cycle formula are proved. Countable finite-energy domination then bounds each descended finite competitor. Passing through the majorants gives the exact envelope downstairs over the arbitrary-cardinality \(k\). This is an actual candidate route, with those operator and descent gates retained. Equality of inverse images of measure supports is neither required nor asserted.

Primary application reads retained for this draft: BJ1801.08229v4 LF2173–2297,2408–2489,2494–2535,2810–2905 and2968–3070; Thuillier tel-00010990v1 LF4800–4902 and3862–3905; Wanner1801.04713v3 LF123–173,735–821 and991–1622 (including the entire pertinent continuous psh-to-subharmonic proof and its model test construction); BE1805.01016v3 LF1710–1788,1820–1995 and2629–2716 (pertinent measure statements and base-extension proof, not the entire proof of Theorem8.4). Entire primary files and all recursively cited proofs have not been read. The separate Bosch–Lütkebohmert cache is machine OCR with previously recorded visual title checks; no new full-proof reading claim is made for it. M1–M3, C1 and C2–C4 are not certified by these observations.


# Older UNREVIEWED finite-obstacle/finite-competitor descent with C1-C4 retained
Binding: 68ec10718968af4c9c656425142fecbd1069338c8287475ce3ee3c09ecec5e2e

# Fiberwise zero class and finite-data envelope descent

Status: new root proposal, not independently reviewed, not an accepted dependency. No original problem is closed by this record. The active original author campaigns P16.1 round 72 and P13.2 round 73 retain their frozen inputs; no hot feedback has been sent.

This proposal concerns projective varieties over algebraically closed trivially valued fields of characteristic zero, with an ample rational line bundle. It does not assert the discrete or arbitrary complete field versions of P16.1/P16.4. Auxiliary complete extensions below are used only to compare values of an already genuine pulled-back metric. They do not supply an affine regularization theorem over arbitrary completed residue fields.

## 1. Exact antecedents and separation of gates

Let X be reduced, irreducible and geometrically unibranch, L ample, and pi: Z -> X a projective resolution factoring through the normalization. The following claims still require reconstruction and independent checking in the current original author work.

F1. For the genuine metric definition being used, a bounded pi*L-psh metric admits decreasing genuine approximants in pi*L + epsilon A, where A is a fixed ample line bundle with an actual semipositive reference metric, epsilon is positive rational and decreases to zero. Pullback of these actual metrics through complete field extension and to an algebraic smooth projective curve preserves their local logarithmic-section subharmonicity. A bounded floor max(u,M), relative to the actual pullback reference, remains genuine. This is a metric assertion, with the actual reference and classes identified; a numerical nef class alone is not a substitute.

F2. Smooth nef envelope theory is available upstairs. Genuine psh functions there are minimum usc extensions from divisorial valuations, and a bounded supremum has unchanged values on that set after usc regularization. The finite morphism from the normalization of X is a universal homeomorphism; pi has geometrically connected fibers.

C1. Over every countable algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field, the preceding singular Runge argument proves weak/genuine local equality and global membership for the descended envelope in the original ample class. This remains the main singular analytic gate. A conditional fiber lemma does not establish C1.

C2. The genuine ample-class theory has decreasing finite Hdom majorants, uniform closure for continuous metrics, decreasing and varying-class closure, the usual PL separation/density, and actual pullback through ground-field extension. Finite Hdom data and the nef inequality defining its dominating class descend through a faithfully flat field extension. In the actual PL/model presentation one retains the relative model and original generic fiber, including nonnormal generic fibers.

C3. The actual continuous mixed MA operator is positive, has raw total mass V=(L^n), and commutes with ground-field extension by pushforward. Its reference and fundamental cycle agree with the genuine operator under consideration. On a model this retains component multiplicities, the factors b_E, and intersection degrees. No formal weak-limit measure is substituted for this operator.

C4. Over countable fields, after C1 gives EP, continuous envelopes satisfy orthogonality. The genuine finite-energy domination principle also holds there. Its hypotheses are u in PSH(L), v in E1(L), with u <= v almost everywhere for MA(v); its conclusion is the pointwise inequality. An envelope is continuous and therefore bounded finite energy. These are additional mathematical inputs to check, not consequences of an arbitrary support claim.

The deductions below are conditional on these precise antecedents. They are intended to remove identified descent defects without concealing the unfinished Runge, operator and variational arguments.

## 2. Correction in the printed zero-class argument

Wu 2609.27610v1, LF 578--588, normalizes phi <= 0 and calls {-m phi} a decreasing sequence. That sequence is increasing, and at a point with phi<0 its limit is positive infinity, not negative infinity. The corrected sequence is {m phi}. BJ 1801.08229v4, LF 2501--2507, uses exactly {m phi}, finite at the trivial valuation and negative infinity at a putative negative divisorial value.

That BJ proof is in its own trivially valued setting. It cannot be applied without explanation to H(x) for an arbitrary analytic x. The next argument instead uses supplied genuine metric approximants and the maximum principle on smooth curves after complete field extension.

## 3. A direct bounded zero-class fiber lemma

Claim, under F1: let F be a complete characteristic-zero field and Y a projective geometrically connected F-scheme. Suppose that a bounded usc function w on Y^an is supplied by a genuine metric on a line bundle trivialized over all of Y, and that the approximants and all base changes/restrictions specified in F1 apply. Then w is constant. We work on Y_red, which has the same analytic points and function values; we do not discard cycle multiplicities in any later MA calculation.

Here is the value comparison, including arbitrary analytic points rather than merely a dense subset. Fix one closed point b of Y_red. For any a in Y^an, embed the finite residue field k(b) into a completed algebraic closure K of H(a). If that field is trivially valued, first extend H(a) to H(a)((T)) with its T-adic absolute value and then take the completed algebraic closure. K is now algebraically closed, complete and nontrivially valued. The H(a)-rational point representing a gives a K-rational lift a', and the chosen embedding gives a K-rational lift b'. Pullback keeps the values w(a')=w(a) and w(b')=w(b).

The scheme (Y_K)_red is connected. Its finitely many irreducible components have a connected intersection graph. Resolve each component projectively, in characteristic zero. The resolutions are smooth projective irreducible K-varieties. Every K-point of a component has a K-point preimage on its resolution: its fiber is nonempty and proper, and K is algebraically closed.

On a smooth projective irreducible K-variety, any two K-points can be joined by a connected chain of smooth projective curves. For positive dimension choose sufficiently high very ample complete intersections through the prescribed finite set, using characteristic-zero Bertini with assigned points; dimension one uses the variety itself, and dimension zero has just one point. Equivalently one can use a finite chain after resolving the selected curves. This algebraic curve-joining statement is an explicit external geometric premise requiring source verification, not an analytic density assertion.

Along every such smooth projective curve C, the pulled-back line bundle from Y is globally trivial and its chosen reference is flat. In a local frame of A let h be the continuous semipositive reference weight. The F1 approximants have local subharmonic weights

    w_i + epsilon_i h,

where w_i decreases to w and epsilon_i decreases to zero. On a relatively compact frame domain, adding a fixed constant to h makes h nonnegative. It then preserves the decreasing order of these local weights. Actual sections give subharmonic logarithmic weights, and finite positive maxima preserve subharmonicity. Thuillier 3.1.8(ii) applies to their decreasing limit. The limit is w, and boundedness excludes the identically negative-infinity alternative. This proves local subharmonicity on C; it assumes the exact F1 reference identification and does not infer it from abstract numerical pullback.

C^an is compact and connected and is a strict smooth analytic curve over K. The bounded usc function w attains a finite maximum M. Thuillier 3.1.11 makes it locally constant near every point where it equals M. The set {w=M} is closed by upper semicontinuity, open by that strong maximum principle, and nonempty. Thus w=M on C^an.

Values consequently agree at the two chosen points on each resolved component, and at closed points of every nonempty intersection of adjacent components. The connected intersection graph gives w(a')=w(b'). Since a was arbitrary, w(a)=w(b) everywhere. Comparing actual values after extension is essential: equality merely on a dense set would not determine an arbitrary usc function.

For the fiber of pi over x in X^an, the line bundle pi*L is the pullback of the one-dimensional H(x)-space L_x. Its reference norm is constant on that entire fiber, so the zero-class trivialization used above is literal. First apply the argument to u_M=max(u,M). Without this floor, a whole component or fiber might carry negative infinity, and the subharmonic definition would not apply. Geometric connectedness is retained under the auxiliary extensions. Each u_M is constant on the fiber. Letting M decrease to negative infinity proves that u itself is constant there, with the value negative infinity allowed.

## 4. Proper birational descent without the flat-stratum lift assertion

Apply section 3 to a genuine upstairs u. Define v(x) to be its common fiber value. Properness and compactness make pi^an closed, and

    {v >= c} = pi^an({u >= c})

for every real c. Hence v is usc and u=v o pi^an. If x is any point and z is any point above it, F2 expresses u(z) as the upper limit of its divisorial values near z. Because pi is birational between integral varieties, its function fields agree. A nonzero divisorial valuation upstairs therefore restricts to the same divisorial valuation downstairs. Thus

    v(x) = u(z) <= limsup_{y -> x, y in X_div} v(y) <= v(x).

This establishes the minimum usc extension property of v. Here the assertion about images of divisorial valuations uses birationality. It is not asserted for arbitrary proper surjective maps.

This avoids Wu LF 605--610's inference that every prescribed net of base divisorial points can be lifted to a convergent net of source divisorial points merely from openness and density. Openness permits lifting nearby points but alone does not supply that stronger divisorial statement. No such statement is needed in this birational argument.

For a bounded-above family {v_i} in PSH_L(X), let

    u = (sup_i pi* v_i)*

on Z. The class pi*L is nef; F2 makes u genuinely pi*L-psh. The family contains a genuine member, so the envelope remains generically finite; this concerns nonidentical negative infinity on irreducible components, not a claim of finiteness at every analytic point. Section 3 gives a descended usc v. At downstairs divisorial points the birational lift is the same valuation, and F2 gives v=sup_i v_i there.

Since every v_i <= v, upper regularization gives (sup_i v_i)* <= v. Conversely v is the minimum usc extension of those divisorial values, while (sup_i v_i)* is usc and majorizes them. Thus v <= (sup_i v_i)*. Equality follows. The proper resolution witnesses weak membership of v in local frames of L. Under C1 the singular local/global argument supplies genuine PSH_L(X), proving countable-field EP. These last genuine-membership steps remain conditional on C1, not on the fiber lemma alone.

## 5. Descending only finite obstacle and competitor data

Now assume countable-field EP in C1, and C2--C4, on the original geometrically unibranch domain. Let k be any algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field. It suffices first to consider a finite rational PL obstacle f on X^an, in an integral or rational ample line-bundle class L.

Choose a countable algebraically closed subfield k0 contained in k over which X, L and the finite presentation of f are defined. All finitely many section coefficients descend. Real additive weights in a finite PL or Hdom formula are retained as real constants in the formula; they do not require field elements. Enlarge k0 by the finite coefficients of the normalization, its birational identification and its finite universal-homeomorphism morphism. Finite type, reducedness, ampleness, normality of that chosen normalization and its finite birational/universal-homeomorphism properties descend by faithful flatness over the perfect field k0. This geometric descent is a further external premise to verify in precisely this form. It preserves the original nonnormal variety; replacing its model by an absolute normalization would alter the generic fiber and is forbidden.

Write r0: X_k^an -> X_0^an for restriction of seminorms. This map is surjective for complete extension of fields; a point has a lift by extending its completed residue-field norm to a suitable completed compositum. Countable EP yields

    phi0 = P_L0(f0), continuous and genuine, with phi0 <= f0.

Set phi=phi0 o r0 and f=f0 o r0. Actual pullback of uniformly approximating genuine metrics makes phi continuous and genuine. It is a feasible envelope competitor. Let nu0 and nu be its actual positive raw MA measures. By C3,

    (r0)_* nu = nu0,       nu(X_k^an)=nu0(X_0^an)=V.

By orthogonality C4, the nonnegative continuous function g0=f0-phi0 has integral zero against nu0. Therefore

    integral (f-phi) dnu = integral (f0-phi0) dnu0 = 0.

Positivity implies f=phi almost everywhere for nu. This does not require equality between the inverse image of Supp(nu0) and Supp(nu). That equality is generally unjustified; the pushforward identity and the integral suffice.

Take a finite actual Hdom competitor h <= f on X_k^an. Its sections, bundles, relative models, finite reference data and nef dominating-class inequality descend to a countable algebraically closed k2 containing k0, under C2. Denote by h2, f2 and phi2 the descended competitor and the pullbacks of f0 and phi0 to X_2^an. Surjectivity of r_{k/k2} transfers h<=f to h2<=f2; genuine membership here is checked from the finite Hdom data and faithful-flat nef descent, not inferred from the pointwise inequality. If actual fixed-L FS competitors are used, basepoint-freeness descends directly by faithful flatness.

C3 transfers orthogonality from k0 to k2 by the same nonnegative integral. Thus h2<=f2=phi2 almost everywhere for MA(phi2). Since phi2 is continuous, it is in the actual E1(L2). C4 domination gives h2<=phi2 everywhere. Pulling back gives h<=phi on X_k^an.

## 6. All genuine competitors, continuity, and EP

No arbitrary PSH competitor is said to descend to one field. Let q be any genuine L-psh competitor with q<=f. By C2 there is a decreasing directed net of finite continuous Hdom majorants h_j tending pointwise to q. For epsilon>0 the closed sets

    K_j = {h_j >= f+epsilon}

are decreasing with empty intersection. Compactness implies K_j is empty for sufficiently large j: otherwise the directed family has the finite intersection property. Consequently h_j-epsilon <= f. It is a finite competitor of the preceding section, so

    q <= h_j <= phi+epsilon.

Let epsilon tend to zero. This proves q<=phi. Feasibility gives the reverse inequality in the envelope definition, hence

    P_L(f) = phi = P_L0(f0) o r0.

The conclusion is continuity and genuine membership, not just agreement on divisorial points or construction of a measure limit.

For continuous obstacles, use density of finite rational PL tests on the compact analytic space and the envelope estimate

    ||P_L(f)-P_L(g)||_infty <= ||f-g||_infty.

Continuous envelopes for PL tests therefore give a continuous envelope for every continuous obstacle; C2 uniform closure gives its genuine membership. For a bounded supremum s* of any genuine family, choose continuous majorants t_j decreasing to s*. Then

    s* <= P_L(t_j) <= t_j,

so P_L(t_j) decreases to s*. The family contains a genuine member, which prevents a componentwise identical negative-infinity limit. C2 decreasing closure proves s* is genuine L-psh. This is the continuity-to-EP implication of BJ 5.17, with its compact-majorant proof made explicit. It never requires the original arbitrary family or its entire supremum to descend.

For a real ample numerical class alpha, choose rational ample omega_j tending to alpha with omega_j-alpha nef. A bounded-above alpha-psh family is also omega_j-psh, since the actual nef reference is retained. Its same usc supremum is omega_j-psh for every j by the rational result. The varying-class closure in C2 makes it alpha-psh. This final numerical-class observation is conditional on the genuine metric/class identification in C2; it does not prove any moving-Reeb strong topology or unbounded moving-class MA convergence.

## 7. What remains before either original problem can be closed

The explicit strict-potential, local hull, integer trace, Hartogs, projection and singular induction arguments in root continuation79 and author round72 must establish C1 without using the desired singular EP as a premise. F1's actual metric conversion and curve restriction, the algebraic curve-joining/geometric-descent premises, C2's finite Hdom/model descent, C3's exact operator identification and C4's domination/orthogonality must be checked. The source snippets below provide evidence with their own hypotheses; they do not silently establish all new applications.

Even a completed EP proof must still be connected to onto normalization pullback, target integrability, actual variational attainment and uniqueness for the unchanged P16.1 equation. For P13.2, a normal rational quotient theorem must additionally yield an actual fixed-irrational-Reeb finite-energy maximizer and the correct original measure image. Uniform root formulas do not imply strong I-completeness. Current approximate solutions and I-Cauchy sequences remain insufficient by themselves.

No acceptance, new solved count, priority claim or formal proof certificate is asserted here.


# Older UNREVIEWED geometric chain, universal-homeomorphism normalization and finite-data models
Binding: 0c0f323068f29b778eb83ad24a0dd976eb1f2215e368bc80a01264105a85c112

# Geometric details for fiber descent and conditional normalization onto

New root reconstruction, not independently reviewed or accepted. It supplements the frozen continuation80 proposal without editing it or either active campaign. The original P16.1 and P13.2 are still open. Retain projective reduced irreducible geometrically unibranch varieties over algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero fields and an ample rational line bundle. Actual metric/curve compatibility F1 and the singular countable Runge/genuine-membership gate C1 remain to establish.

## 1. The resolution has geometrically connected fibers

Let nu: Xn -> X be the normalization and p: Z -> Xn a projective resolution. Finite-type schemes over a field have finite normalization. Geometric unibranchness makes nu a universal homeomorphism; this is [Stacks, Tag 0GIQ](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0GIQ), with its local normalization-ring proof read. For normal X the same conclusion follows from the local normal-domain definition; see [Tag 0BQ3](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BQ3).

Apply [Tag 0AY8](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0AY8) to p. The target Xn is normal and integral, Z is integral and reduced, p is proper, its generic point dominates the target, and the birational generic fiber is Spec k(Xn), whose global functions are exactly k(Xn). All six hypotheses are therefore satisfied. Stein factorization gives geometrically connected fibers of p. The finite intermediate algebra is a reduced integral algebra with the same function field as Xn; normality forces it to equal O_Xn.

For any field-valued point x of X, the normalization fiber is a finite universal-homeomorphism scheme over k(x). Its reduction is a single point with a purely inseparable residue extension. In characteristic zero that extension is the identity. After any further field extension the same conclusion holds. The reduced fiber of pi=nu p consequently has the same underlying space as the corresponding p fiber. Nilpotent thickenings do not change analytic points. Thus pi has geometrically connected fibers also over arbitrary completed residue fields H(x). This concerns fiber topology; it does not discard multiplicities in the MA fundamental cycle.

## 2. Joining two values uses ordinary Bertini on a blowup

Let K be algebraically closed of characteristic zero and Y smooth projective irreducible over K, of dimension d. To compare values at two K-points a,b, equality is immediate if a=b or d=0; for d=1 use Y itself. Assume d>=2 and a!=b.

Blow up the two distinct smooth points. The resulting Y' is smooth projective irreducible. Its exceptional divisors Ea and Eb are projective (d-1)-spaces. Choose a very ample H on Y'. Successively choose d-1 general members of H, keeping every intermediate intersection smooth and connected and meeting each exceptional divisor properly. Ordinary smooth Bertini applies because the complete linear system generates H and embeds Y'; its exact hypotheses and the dimension-count proof are [Tag 0FD6](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FD6). At each stage of dimension at least two, connectedness follows from depth at least two, properness and ampleness in [Tag 0FD9](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0FD9). Smooth connected schemes over K are irreducible here. The combined argument is also recorded in [Tag 0G4G](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0G4G).

The additional proper-intersection requirements exclude only finitely many proper closed subsets of each hyperplane parameter space, so they can be imposed with the smoothness requirements. The final C is a smooth projective connected curve. Because H restricts amply to each Ei,

    length(C intersection Ei) = (H^(d-1) . Ei) > 0.

It therefore has a K-point above a and another above b. The composed algebraic morphism C -> Y joins the original points. No linear system with assigned base points was used.

For a connected reduced projective scheme, resolve its finitely many irreducible components, choose preimages of the two points and of closed points in adjacent component intersections, and apply this construction on each resolution. Its component-intersection graph is connected, so finitely many curve comparisons connect the two values. The curves need only map to the original scheme. Consequently F1 must mean pullback of supplied actual metric approximants along algebraic morphisms from smooth curves, including the resolution and blowup maps; it must not be read as just restriction along smooth closed embeddings. Pullback of actual generating sections preserves generation and supplies that functorial route once F1's metric/class identification has been proved.

## 3. Finite normalization data really can be descended

Let k0 be the algebraic closure in k of a field generated by finitely many coefficients of X, L and a finite PL test. It is countable. Enlarge this finite coefficient set by affine presentations of the finite normalization, multiplication tables, monic integral equations for its generators, overlap maps, and the birational inverse on one nonempty affine open. These are finitely many data. The resulting map B0 over A0 is finite and birational, with its base change the original normalization. The original variety and its generic fiber are retained.

Reducedness descends because A0 -> A0 tensor k is injective. Normality of B0 descends by [Tag 033G](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/033G). Locally an element a/b integral over a downstairs domain lies in its upstairs normal extension. Faithful flatness contracts bS to bR, so a/b was downstairs already. Since B0 is finite birational and normal, it is precisely the integral closure of A0: an element integral over A0 is also integral over B0 in their common fraction field.

The descended finite map is a universal homeomorphism by fpqc descent, whose exact statement and proof were read at [Tag 0CEX](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0CEX). Thus each local integral closure has one maximal ideal and purely inseparable residue extension. This is geometric unibranchness by [Tag 0BPZ](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BPZ). It proves that the countable descended variety satisfies the geometric hypothesis required by C1. The proof does not assume that arbitrary PSH functions descend.

Finite Hdom nef dominance has an especially direct downward check over the algebraically closed fields being used. Clear denominators in the rational difference D=L-M. Every integral proper curve C over the smaller algebraically closed field is geometrically integral; its base extension stays integral. Cohomology under field extension gives the same Euler characteristic and hence the same degree of D. If D is nef upstairs, its degree on every such downstairs curve is nonnegative. Thus D is nef downstairs. Finite generation of the section presentation descends by faithful flatness. This verifies downward finite Hdom data, while actual inclusion in the genuine class still uses the nef-reference/model compatibility in C2. No ascent of arbitrary model regularity or absolute normalization is inferred.

## 4. A finite birational normalization is an actual blowup

This provides a concrete route into BJ's birational PSH theorem after EP, not a substitute for EP. Let B=nu_*O_Xn. It is a finite O_X-algebra inside the common rational function field. The conductor J=Ann(B/O_X) is a nonzero coherent ideal. For a sufficiently high ample twist A choose a generically nonzero section a of J tensor A. Locally trivialize A and let a_i be the resulting nonzero element of J. The ideals

    I_i = a_i B_i subset O_X(U_i)

glue to a coherent ideal I: on overlaps the a_i differ by an invertible regular transition factor. Each I_i is finite and generically trivial. The multiplication of B_i gives I_i^m=a_i^m B_i for every m>=1.

On U_i=Spec A_i the Rees algebra is A_i in degree zero and a_i^m B_i in positive degree m. Its Proj is covered by the single chart D_+(a_i t). Indeed, for b in B_i a monic integral equation over A_i, multiplied by the appropriate powers of a_i t, makes (a_i b t) integral over the graded subalgebra generated by a_i t. A homogeneous prime containing a_i t must therefore contain every positive-degree generator, and cannot be a Proj point. The degree-zero part of the localized Rees algebra is

    A_i[I_i/a_i] = B_i.

Hence Bl_I X is exactly Xn, compatibly on overlaps. This makes the ideal needed for the error-term argument explicit, without changing the variety or passing to a different hull.

## 5. Conditional onto PSH after the envelope gate

Suppose EP has been proved in every rational ample class of X by C1 and the finite-data extension. Put theta=c1(L) and theta_m=(1+1/m)theta. Then theta_m-theta is ample, the theta_m have EP, and theta_m decreases to theta.

BJ 1801.08229v4 LF2557--2594 proves the birational error theorem: for the blowup ideal I there is an ample class omega and h=log|I| in PSH(omega) such that any psi in PSH(pi*theta) satisfies

    psi + epsilon pi*h = pi* v_epsilon,

with v_epsilon in PSH(theta+epsilon omega). Choose epsilon_m decreasing to zero, small enough that theta_m-theta-epsilon_m omega is nef. Since h<=0, the v_m increase on divisorial valuations and therefore everywhere by the minimum usc extension theorem. Their sup normalization is bounded: h has zero value at the trivial valuation. For each m0, EP in theta_m0 supplies the increasing-net limit as a genuine theta_m0-psh function. Its divisorial values are independent of m0. Varying-class closure then puts that same minimum usc function v in PSH(theta), and divisorial uniqueness yields pi*v=psi. This is the actual onto conclusion of BJ Lemma 5.13, LF2859--2873.

The source's final line LF2594 prints PSH(theta) after proving membership in PSH(L_m+H). In that error theorem the class there must be theta+omega, as the theorem statement and every preceding line require. The ample error is retained in the preceding argument and is sent to zero only in the later EP-based step. Dropping it at LF2594 would incorrectly assert unconditional onto invariance.

Applied to nu, the analytic map is a homeomorphism: each completed-residue-field fiber is finite, local and radicial, with one analytic point, and properness gives a closed continuous surjection of compact Hausdorff spaces. Thus onto PSH also gives onto CPSH, using actual continuity and the inverse homeomorphism.

To extend this to E1 and the equation one still must verify the proper projection formula for the actual continuous mixed operator, nu_*[Xn]=[X], degree-one raw mass equality and decreasing unbounded energy/MA compatibility. With those identities, the continuous-majorant definitions on the two spaces correspond exactly, giving energy equality and E1 onto; finite-energy target measures correspond by the homeomorphism. These analytic operator identities and the actual variational existence argument are deliberately retained as requirements, not declared solved by this geometric addendum.

## 6. Read evidence and remaining limit

The linked Stacks statement/proof bodies were actually read through the native web tool on 5 October 2026. These are live scholarly pages; their stable tags are cited, but no full primary-file hash or LF snapshot is asserted. The explanations above are new paraphrased derivations with their application hypotheses, not copied page bodies. Local BJ evidence is the frozen extract with SHA256 659bb8d2e5335781041f03cdb168bac70126487bc0db394f3dac8cf1e9f076d0, read at inclusive LF2550--2616 and previously LF2859--2881.

This addendum supplies checked primary geometric inputs and an explicit conditional normalization route. It does not establish F1 metric conversion, C1 singular Runge/genuine membership, or the remaining equation theorem. No original closure or accepted new dependency is asserted.

      "literal": "Notation and conventions.\n     • We use the standard abbreviations usc for ‘upper semicontinuous’, lsc for ‘lower\n       semicontinuous’, wlog for ‘without loss of generality”, and iff for ‘if and only if’.\n       We also use PL for ‘piecewise linear’, snc for ‘simple normal crossing’, and psh for\n       ‘plurisubharmonic’.\n     • Following the Bourbaki convention, all compact and locally compact topological\n       spaces are required to be Hausdorff.\n     • A net in a set X is a family (xi )i∈I of elements of X indexed by a directed set, i.e.\n       a partially preordered set in which any two elements are dominated by a third one.\n       A subnet of (xi )i∈I is a net of the form (xϕ(j) )j∈J where ϕ : J → I is increasing and\n       final, i.e. for each i ∈ I there exists j ∈ J with ϕ(j) ≥ i. A (Hausdorff) topological\n       space X is compact iff every net in X admits a convergent subnet.\n     • If X is a Hausdorff topological space, and ϕ : X → R ∪ {±∞} is any function, then\n       the usc regularization ϕ? of ϕ is the smallest usc function with ϕ? ≥ ϕ. Concretely,\n       ϕ? (x) = lim supy→x ϕ(y). The lsc regularization is defined by ϕ? = −(−ϕ)? .\n     • We work over an algebraically closed field k, for the most part of arbitrary charac-\n       teristic. In this paper, a variety (over k) is a separated k-scheme of finite type that\n       is reduced, but not necessarily irreducible, nor even equidimensional.\n\f10                      SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON\n\n     • For any variety X, we denote by Pic(X) the Picard group of isomorphism classes of\n       line bundles. Elements of the associated Q-vector space Pic(X)Q := Pic(X) ⊗Z Q\n       can be viewed as isomorphism classes of Q-line bundles.\n     • If X is a projective variety, we denote by N1 (X) the finite dimensional R-vector\n       space of numerical classes of R-Cartier divisors on X. It comes with a surjective\n       linear map Pic(X)R → N1 (X) induced by L 7→ c1 (L) .\n     • Ample classes form a nonempty open convex cone Amp(X) ⊂ N1 (X), whose closure\n       is the closed convex cone Nef(X) ⊂ N1 (X) of nef classes. For θ, θ0 ∈ N1 (X), we\n       write θ ≥ θ0 if θ − θ0 is nef. We generally denote by θ an element of N1 (X), and by\n       ω an element of Amp(X).\n     • The cone Psef(X) ⊂ N1 (X) of pseudoeffective classes is defined as the closed convex\n       cone generated by classes of effective R-Cartier divisors; its interior Big(X) is the\n       cone of big classes.\n     • A section of a line bundle on X is regular if it does not vanish identically along any\n       irreducible component of X.       "literal": "Definition 1.10. We say that a test configuration X for X is integrally closed if the scheme\nX is integrally closed in the generic fiber Xk($) of π : X → A1 .\n  In other words, X is integrally closed iff it can be covered by affine open subsets U =\nSpec A such that the k[$]-algebra A is integrally closed in Ak($) . Note that X is normal iff\nX is normal and X is integrally closed.\nExample 1.11. The trivial test configuration Xtriv = X × A1 is integrally closed.\n   The integral closure of any test configuration X in Xk($) defines a finite, Gm -equivariant\nmorphism Xe → X (because the scheme X , being of finite type over a field, is excellent),\nwhich induces an isomorphism on the generic fibers over A1 . Thus Xe is an integrally closed\ntest configuration for X, which we simply call the integral closure of X . It is characterized\nas the unique integrally closed test configuration that dominates X via a finite morphism.\n   When X is normal, the integral closure Xe of any test configuration X coincides with its\nnormalization X ν (see also Remark 1.22).\nLemma 1.12 (Zariski’s main theorem). If X is integrally closed and µ : X 0 → X is a\nmorphism of test configurations, then µ? OX 0 = OX .\nProof. By coherence of direct images, µ? OX 0 is a finite OX -module. Sections of µ? OX 0 on\nan open U ⊂ X are thus rational functions on U that are regular on the generic fiber and\nintegral over OU , and hence belong to OU .                                                \u0003\n  Integral closedness admits the following characterization, a ‘vertical version’ of the usual\nSerre criterion for normality.\nTheorem 1.13. A test configuration X is integrally closed iff it is:\n   (i) vertically R1 , in the sense that X is regular at each generic points of X0 ;\n  (ii) vertically S2 , in the sense that depth OX ,ξ ≥ min{2, dim OX ,ξ } for all ξ ∈ X0 .\nRemark 1.14. Condition (i) was called partially normal in [Oda13, Definition 3.7].\nLemma 1.15. For any test configuration X for X, we have:\n   (i) X is vertically S2 ⇐⇒ X0 is S1 , i.e. without embedded points;\n  (ii) X is S2 ⇐⇒ X is S2 and X is vertically S2 .\n\f             GLOBAL PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY OVER A TRIVIALLY VALUED FIELD                       17\n\nProof. Since X0 is a Cartier divisor, each ξ ∈ X0 satisfies\n                depth OX ,ξ = depth OX0 ,ξ + 1,    dim OX ,ξ = dim OX0 ,ξ + 1.\nThis yields (i), while (ii) is a direct consequence of the isomorphism\n                                      X \\ X0 ≃ X × Gm .\n                                                                                              \u0003\nCorollary 1.16. For any test configuration X , the following are equivalent:\n   (i) X0 is reduced;\n  (ii) X is integrally closed, and X0 is generically reduced.\nProof. The scheme X0 is reduced iff it is generically reduced and S1 . By Lemma 1.15, X0\nis thus reduced iff it is generically reduced and X is vertically S2 . Finally, X0 generically\nreduced implies that X is regular at each generic point of X0 , since the latter is a Cartier\ndivisor. The result is now a consequence of Theorem 1.13.                                   \u0003\n  Before entering the proof of Theorem 1.13, we introduce some terminology that will be\nused throughout this paper.\nDefinition 1.17. Let X be a test configuration for X.\n    (i) A vertical fractional ideal a on X is a coherent fractional ideal sheaf that is Gm -\n        invariant, and trivial outside X0 .\n   (ii) The polar scheme P ⊂ X of a vertical fractional ideal a is the closed subscheme of\n        X defined by the ideal of poles p := {f ∈ OX | f a ⊂ OX }.\n  (iii) A vertical Cartier divisor D on X is a Gm -invariant Cartier divisor with support in\n        X0 .\n   Note that P in (ii) is supported in X0 , a being trivial outside X0 .\n   Vertical Cartier divisors on X are in 1–1 correspondence with locally principal vertical\nfractional ideals of X , via D 7→ OX (D). If D is further effective, then it coincides with the\npolar scheme of OX (D).\nLemma 1.18. A test configuration X is integrally closed iff every vertical fractional ideal\na on X that is integral over OX satisfies a ⊂ OX .\nProof. The ‘only if’ part is obvious. Conversely, consider the integral closure µ : Xe → X .\nThen a := µ? OXe is a vertical fractional ideal that is integral over OX , and the ‘if part’\nfollows.                                                                                  \u0003\n  The next result is the key step in the proof of Theorem 1.13.\nLemma 1.19. Let X be an integrally closed test configuration for X, and a be a vertical\nfractional ideal on X . Then:\n     (i) every associated point of the scheme of poles of a is a generic point of X0 ;\n    (ii) X is vertically R1 .\nProof. We follow the usual proof of Serre’s criterion for normality. Let p = {f ∈ OX |\nf a ∈ OX } be the ideal of poles, and pick an associated point ξ ∈ X0 of the polar scheme\nP ⊂ X . Then f mξ · aξ is an ideal of OX ,ξ , and hence\nf mξ ·aξ ⊂ mξ or f mξ ·aξ = OX ,ξ . In the former case, the usual determinant trick implies that\n\f18                       SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON\n\nf aξ is integral over OX ,ξ . Since X is integrally closed, we infer f aξ ⊂ OX ,ξ , i.e. f ∈ pξ , a\ncontradiction. We thus necessarily have f mξ · aξ = OX ,ξ , which proves that mξ is invertible,\nand hence that OX ,ξ is a DVR. Consider the\ndecreasing sequence (mϕ)m∈N and its pointwise limit ψ := limm (mϕ). Since ψ(vtriv ) = 0,\nwe have ψ ∈ PSH(0) (see Theorem 4.5). Envelopes. For ample classes, the envelope property admits a useful reformulation.\nWe continue to assume that X is irreducible unless stated otherwise. Fix a class ω ∈\nAmp(X).\nDefinition 5.16. The ω-psh envelope of a function ϕ : X an → R ∪ {±∞} is the function\nPω (ϕ) : X an → R ∪ {±∞} defined as the pointwise supremum\n                           Pω (ϕ) := sup {ψ ∈ PSH(ω) | ψ ≤ ϕ} .\n  Thus Pω (ϕ) ≡ −∞ iff there is no ψ ∈ PSH(ω) with ψ ≤ ϕ.       "literal": "   We will first introduce semipositive formal metrics. We have seen in Proposition 2.10 that\nformal metrics are the same as piecewise linear metrics and hence everything applies to piecewise\nlinear metrics as well.\n3.1. Let X be a proper scheme over K with a line bundle L over X. We call an algebraic\nK ◦ -model (X , L ) of (X, L) numerically effective (briefly nef) if degL (C) ≥ 0 for every closed\ncurve C in X which is proper over K ◦ . Of course, properness implies that C is contained in\nthe special fiber Xs . An algebraic metric k k on Lan is said to be semipositive if there is a nef\nalgebraic K ◦ -model (X , L ) of (X, L) such that k k = k kL . We say that a line bundle L on\nX is numerically trivial if degL (C) = 0 for every closed curve C in X which is proper over\nK ◦ . Equivalently, we can require that L and L −1 are both nef. We say that L is numerically\nequivalent to a line bundle L ′ on X if L ′ ⊗ L −1 is numerically trivial.\n3.2. The above definition is easily generalized to the analytic setting: Let L be a line bundle on\na paracompact strictly K-analytic space V . A formal K ◦ -model (V, L) of (V, L) is called nef if\ndegL (C) ≥ 0 for any closed curve C in the special fiber Vs which is proper over the residue field\nK̃. A formal metric k k on L is called semipositive if there is a nef formal K ◦ -model (V, L) of\n(V, L) such that k k = k kL . Obviously, the trivial metric on OV is a semipositive formal metric.\n   It will follow from Proposition 3.5 below that we may use any model to test semipositivity of\nthe associated metrics. Based on this result, it is easy to check that the tensor product of two\nsemipositive formal metrics is again a semipositive formal metric.\nLemma 3.3. This is\nproven in the projective case in [EFM, Remark 1.3.25] and the proper case follows from Chow’s\nlemma and the projection formula.                                                               \u0003\nLemma 3.4. Let V be a paracompact strictly K-analytic space, L a line bundle on V and (V, L)\na formal K ◦ -model of (V, L). Let (Vred , Lred ) be the formal K ◦ -model of (Vred , Lred) obtained by\nputting the induced reduced structure. Then L is nef if and only if Lred is nef.\nProof. Let Vred be the induced reduced structure on V. Since Vred → V is finite (in fact a\nclosed immersion), we deduce that the induced map (Vred )s → Vs between the special fibers is\nfinite. By the projection formula, we conclude that L is nef if and only if Lred is nef.   \u0003\n\f                               ON ZHANG’S SEMIPOSITIVE METRICS                                     13\n\n\nProposition 3.5.       "literal": "     In view of Theorem 7.1, it is natural to introduce as in [BJ18a]:\nDefinition 7.2. Assume that K is non-Archimedean, and let L be a semiample Q-line\nbundle. We say that a (possibly singular) metric φ on L is plurisubharmonic ( psh for short)\nif φ can be written as the pointwise limit of a decreasing net φi ∈ FS(L) of Fubini–Study\nmetrics on L. We denote by PSH(L) the set of psh metrics on L.\n   Given a subgroup Γ ⊂ R, nontrivial if K is trivially valued, FSΓ (L) is dense in FS(L)\nwith respect to uniform convergence, by Proposition 5.4 (v); it would thus be equivalent to\nrequire φi ∈ FSΓ (L) in the above definition.\n  As a direct consequence of Corollary 5.7, we have:\nLemma 7.3. The space PSH(L) is stable under finite max and decreasing limits.\nCorollary 7.6. Pick a subgroup Γ ⊂ R, nontrivial if K is trivially valued. For any semi-\nample Q-line bundle L, the set C0 ∩ PSH(L) of continuous psh metrics on L is the closure\nof FSΓ (L) with respect to uniform convergence.\nProof. Let φ be a continuous psh metric, and pick a decreasing net (φi ) in FSΓ (L) converging\npointwise to φ. By Dini’s lemma, φi → φ uniformly on X an , and φ is thus in the closure\nof FSΓ (L). Conversely, let (φi ) be a sequence in FSΓ (L) converging unifornly to some\n(continuous) metric φ. We can inductively construct a sequence of constants ci → 0 such\nthat φi + ci is decreasing, and it follows that φ = limi (φi + ci ) is psh.                  \u0003\n\f                NORMS, DETERMINANT OF COHOMOLOGY AND FEKETE POINTS                           49\n\n7.2. Nef metrics. Assume that K is non-Archimedean. A model metric φ on a Q-line\nbundle L is nef if φ = φL for some Q-model L of L which is nef, i.e. L · C ≥ 0 for each\ne\nK-projective  curve C ⊂ Xs . By [GM19, Proposition 3.5], any Q-model L′ of L such that\nφ = φL′ is then also nef.\nDefinition 7.7. A nef metric on a Q-line bundle L is a continuous metric φ that can be\nwritten as a uniform limit of nef model metrics.\n   This is usually known as a semipositive metric in the sense of S.W. Zhang, cf. [GM19] for\na thorough discussion. When K is trivially valued, the trivial metric is the only nef metric.\nTheorem 7.8. Assume that K is nontrivially valued, and let L be an ample Q-line bundle.\nThen a continuous metric on L is nef iff it is psh (in the sense of Definition 7.2).\n   A proof of this result is also sketched, in a slightly different language, in [CMor18, Remark\n3.18]; we nevertheless provide a proof below, for completeness. By [GM19, Corollary 1.4],\nwe get:\nCorollary 7.9. Let L be an ample Q-line bundle, and pick a Q-model L of L. Then φL is\npsh iff L is nef.\nProof of Theorem 7.8. By Theorem 5.14, pure Fubini–Study metrics on L coincide with\nmodel metrics associated to Q-models L of L that are semiample, and hence nef. Since K\nis nontrivially valued, Corollary 7.6 implies that every continuous psh metric is a uniform\nlimit of pure Fubini–Study metrics, and hence is nef.\n   To prove the converse, let L be a nef Q-model of L, determined on a model X of X.\nUsing again Theorem 5.14, it will be enough to show that φL is a uniform limit of model\nmetrics associated to semiample Q-models. Since L is ample, it admits an ample Q-model\nA on some projective model X ′ dominating X (cf. [GM19, Lemma 4.12]). The pull-back\nL′ of L to X ′ is nef. For each ε ∈ (0, 1) ∩ Q, Lε := (1 − ε)L′ + εH is thus ample on Xs ,\nand hence on X , by [EGA, IV.9.6.4]. In particular, Lε is a semiample model of L. Since\nφL = φL′ (Lemma 5.10), φLε − φL = ε(φH − φL ) converges to 0 uniformly as ε > 0, and we\nare done.                                                                                 \u0003\nRemark 7.10. We expect Theorem 7.8 and Corollary 7.9 to hold when L is merely semi-\nample. For each trivializing section τ of L over an open U ⊂ X, the function\nlog |τ |φ is smooth, and ddc φ = −ddc log |τ |φ on U an . Again, this makes sense for Q-line\nbundles as well.\nDefinition 7.11.       "literal": "    In the theory of Chambert-Loir and Ducros [CD12, §5.4], we can associate to every\ncontinuous real function u on X an associated current [u]. The function h is called\nplurisubharmonic (or just psh) in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros if the associated\ncurrent d′ d′′ [h] is positive. See [CD12, §5.5] for details. Then we can show the following\nmaximum principle.\nTheorem 1.7. Let X be a good, separated, strictly k-analytic space, and let u be a piecewise\nsmooth function on X which is psh in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros. If u has a\nlocal maximum on x ∈ X \\ ∂ X , then u is locally constant at x.\n   Since any semipositive R-PL function is psh in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros\n[GJR21, Theorem 7.14], the maximum principle holds for semipositive R-PL functions.\nWe prove Theorem 1.7 by proving a tropical analogue in Theorem 7.4: namely, if C ⊂\nRn is a tropical variety of pure dimension d and if f : C → R is weakly tropically psh,\nin the sense that the Lagerberg current d′ d′′ [ f ] is positive, then f satisfies a similar\nmaximum principle. The proof uses the Bieri–Groves theorem for affinoid varieties given\nin Theorem 1.6. Then Theorem 1.7 is shown in Theorem 7.7.\nRemark 1.8.       "literal": "                             5. SEMIPOSITIVE R-PL FUNCTIONS\n   Let k be a non-trivially valued non-Archimedean field. We consider a good, strictly\nk-analytic space X . We refer to 2.5 for the definition of PL functions. Recall from the in-\ntroduction that an R-PL function u: X → R is called semipositive at x ∈ X if the associated\nresidue line bundle Lu (x) ∈ Pic(red(X , x))R is nef, where we refer to [GJR21, 6.16–6.19]\nfor residue line bundles. Equivalently, there is a strictly affinoid neighbourhood U of x\nsuch that u = hL for a formal k◦ -model (U, L) of (U, OU ) with L nef [GJR21, Proposition\n7.1].\n   We will show first that for an R-PL function on a smooth curve, semipositivity is the\nsame as subharmonicity in the sense of Thuillier’s thesis [Thu05, Définition 3.1.5]. Re-\ncall that Thuillier defines a subharmonic function on a smooth k-analytic curve X as a\nsemicontinuous function u: X → R ∪ {−∞} which is not identically −∞ on any con-\nnected component of X and which has the property that for every harmonic function h\non a strictly affinoid domain U of X with u|∂ U ≤ h|∂ U , we have u|U ≤ h.\n\nProposition 5.1. Let X be a smooth, strictly k-analytic curve and let u: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. Then u is subharmonic at x ∈ X in the sense of Thuillier if and only if Lu (x) is nef.\n\nProof. First we treat the case when k is algebraically closed. As explained in 2.5, there\nexists a compact strictly analytic neighborhood U of x in X , a formal k◦ -model U of U,\nand a formal model L of OU such that u|U = hL . Since k is algebraically closed, we may\nassume that the special fiber Us is reduced, which implies that U is covered by formal\naffines of the form Spf(A ◦ ) for a strictly k-affinoid algebra A [GJR21, §2.4]. It follows\nfrom [Ber90, Proposition 2.4.4] that for every irreducible component Y of Us , there is a\nunique point ξY ∈ U reducing to the generic point of Y . In the formalism of real valued\nforms and currents on Berkovich spaces [CD12], it is well known that d′ d′′ [u]|U is the\ndiscrete measure on U supported on the points ξY of U for Y an irreducible component\nof Us , with multiplicity at ξY equal to degL (Y ). See [CD12, §6.9] for a more general\nstatement. Wanner showed that subharmonicity of u on U is equivalent to the positivity\nof the current d′ d′′ [u]: see [Wan19, Theorems 1 and 2] and use the fact that X is locally\nalgebraic [vdP80]. Thus Ls is nef if and only if u is subharmonic on U. Suppose that u is\nsubharmonic at x. Shrinking U, we may assume that u is subharmonic on U. This implies\nthat Ls is nef, so it follows from [GJR21, Proposition 7.1] that Lu (x) is nef. Conversely, if\nLu (x) is nef, then by [GJR21, Proposition 7.1], we may shrink U so that Ls is nef, which\nas above implies that u is subharmonic on U.\n   It remains to show that subharmonicity of u at x and numerical effectivity of Lu (x)\ncan be checked after extending scalars to the completion of an algebraic closure K =\n(k)∧ . By [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2)], one can check that Lh (x) is nef after base change\nto K. The same is true for subharmonicity: this follows directly from Thuillier’s definition\nand the fact that the base change of a harmonic function remains harmonic [Thu05,\nProposition 2.3.18].                                                                       \u0003\n\n  Our next goal is a characterisation of semipositive R-PL functions by restriction to\nsubcurves as in the complex case. We need the following characterization of boundary\npoints:\n\f16                                W. GUBLER AND J. RABINOFF\n\n5.2. Let X be an admissible formal k◦ -scheme and let x ∈ Xη . Then x ∈ Int(X ) = X \\ ∂ X\nif and only if the closure of redX (x) is a proper ek-scheme. This follows from [CD12,\nLemme 6.5.1] in the separated case and from [Vil21, Corollary A.4] in general.\nTheorem 5.3. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space, and let h: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. For x ∈ X , the following conditions are equivalent.\n    (1) The function h is semipositive at x.\n    (2) There is a neighborhood W of x such for every strictly analytic neighborhood U ⊂ W\n        of x and every Zariski-closed curve C in U, the restriction h|C is semipositive.\nProof. Since semipositivity is an open property that is stable under pullback [GJR21,\nProposition 7.6], it is clear that (1) yields (2).\n   Assume that h satisfies (2). By shrinking W , we may assume that W is a strictly affinoid\nneighborhood of x and that there is a formal model W of U and a model L of OW on W\nwith coefficients in R such that h = hL on W . Let Y ⊂ Ws be a curve which is proper over\nthe residue field ek. To prove semipositivity at x, it is enough to show that degL (Y ) ≥ 0.\n   Choose any point y ∈ W reducing to the generic point η of Y . By Theorem 4.1 as\napplied to X = W and X = W, there exist z ∈ W with redW (z) = η, a neighborhood U\nof z in W , and an irreducible 1-dimensional Zariski-closed subspace C of U containing\nz. By shrinking U, we may assume that U is strictly affinoid. By results of Bosch and\nLütkebohmert [Bos14, Theorem 8.4.3] [BL93b, Corollary 5.4(b)], there is a formal model\nU of U and a morphism π: U → W extending the inclusion U ,→ W on generic fibers. Let\nC be the formal closure of C in U. This is an admissible formal model of C whose special\nfiber Cs is a closed 1-dimensional subscheme of Us containing η′ = redU (z). Functoriality\nof reductions shows that π(η′ ) = η, so the closure Y ′ of η′ is a curve mapping to Y .\nSince the closure of η in Ws is the proper curve Y , we conclude from 5.2 that z ∈ W \\\n∂ W . The boundary ∂ U is the union of ∂ W ∩ U with the topological boundary of U in\nW [Ber90, Proposition 3.1.3], so since U is a neighborhood of z in W , we have z ∈ U \\∂ U.\nApplying 5.2 to U and its formal model U shows that Y ′ is proper as well. Therefore,\nthe map Y ′ → Y is finite and surjective. Note that Y ′ is an irreducible component of Cs .\nApplying (2) to U and C, we have that h|C is semipositive, and so we deduce from [GJR21,\nLemma 7.4] as applied to (π∗L)|C that degπ∗ L (Y ′ ) ≥ 0. The projection formula then shows\ndegL (Y ) ≥ 0, as required.                                                                \u0003\n   To apply results from Thuillier’s thesis [Thu05], we would like to use only smooth an-\nalytic curves. To give a variant of the above result suitable for this purpose, we introduce\nthe following notations:\n   For a real function h on X and an analytic field extension F of k, we denote by h F\nthe induced function on the base change X F = X ⊗     b k F of X . We call an R-PL function\nh pluriharmonic if ±h are both semipositive. A strictly k-analytic space X is called rig-\nsmooth if the sheaf of Kähler differentials, considered on the G-topology, is locally free of\nrank equal to the local dimension. The space X is smooth if and only if X is rig-smooth and\nboundaryless. See [Duc18] for details (Ducros calls rig-smooth spaces quasi-smooth).\nCorollary 5.4. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space and let h: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. Then the following conditions are equivalent:\n\f     ON SEMIPOSITIVE PIECEWISE LINEAR FUNCTIONS IN NON-ARCHIMEDEAN ANALYTIC GEOMETRY        17\n\n   (1) The function h is semipositive (resp. pluriharmonic).\n   (2) There is an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k such that for every\n       rig-smooth analytic curve C over F and every morphism ϕ : C → X F , the function\n       h F ◦ ϕ is semipositive (resp. harmonic) on C.\n   (3) There is an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k such that for every\n       smooth analytic curve C over F and every morphism ϕ : C → X F , the function h F ◦ϕ\n       is semipositive (resp. harmonic) on C.\n\n   If (2) or (3) is true for an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k, then\nit is true for all analytic field extensions. This follows from Corollary 5.4 and Propo-\nsition [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2’)] which allows to check semipositivity over any field\nextension.\n\nProof. It is enough to prove the claim for semipositive functions, as h is pluriharmonic if\nand only if h and −h are semipositive. Since semipositivity is stable under pullback and\nextension of scalars [GJR21, Proposition 7.6], clearly (1) yields (2). Since a smooth an-\nalytic curve is just a boundaryless rig-smooth curve, it is clear that (2) yields (3). In fact,\nthese two conditions are equivalent as an R-PL function is automatically semipositive in\nevery boundary point y of an analytic curve C. Indeed, it follows from 5.2 that red(C, y)\nis non-proper and hence there is no condition for semipositivity at y.\n   It remains to show that that (2) yields (1). Assume that (2) holds. By base change\nusing [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2’)], we may assume that k = F . Let x ∈ X and let U be\na strictly analytic neighborhood of x. For any Zariski-closed curve Y in U, we will show\nthat h|Y is semipositive. Replacing Y by Yred and using [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(3’)], we\nmay assume that Y is reduced. Let ϕ : C → Y be the normalization of Y (see [Ber90]\nbefore Proposition 3.1.8). Then C is a rig-smooth curve because k is perfect (it is alge-\nbraically closed). By (2), the function h ◦ ϕ is semipositive on C. Since the normalization\nmorphism is finite and surjective, it follows from [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(1’)] that h|Y is\nsemipositive. By Theorem 5.3, we conclude that h is semipositive at x. Since x was an\narbitrary point of X , we get (1).                                                           \u0003\nCorollary 5.5.       "literal": "Théorème 3.2. Soit X un espace k-analytique compact et Γ-strict de dimension d,\net soit f = (f1 , . . . , fn ) une famille de fonctions analytiques inversibles sur X ;\nnotons |f | l’application (|f1 |, . . . , |fn |) de X vers (R∗+ )n . Posons c = (Q, (|k ∗ | · Γ)Q ).\n1) L’image |f |(X) est un c-polytope de (R∗+ )n , qui est de dimension inférieure ou\n   égale à d.\n2) L’image |f |(∂X) est contenue dans un c-polytope de (R∗+ )n qui est de dimension\n   inférieure ou égale à d − 1 ; lorsque X est aﬃnoı̈de |f |(∂X) est elle-même un c-\n   polytope de (R∗+ )n de dimension inférieure ou égale à d − 1.\n    Démonstration. La seconde assertion se déduira de la première.\n(3.2.1) Preuve de 1) : réduction à une situation algébrique. On peut supposer\nque X est aﬃnoı̈de et irréductible, et l’on procède alors par récurrence sur d. Si d = 0\nil n’y a rien à démontrer. Supposons que d > 0 et que 1) est vraie en dimension < d.\n     Le théorème à établir est purement topologique. On peut donc supposer k parfait\n(en étendant les scalaires au complété de la clôture radicelle de k), et X réduit (en\nle remplaçant par Xred ). L’espace X est alors génériquement quasi-lisse. Soit Y son\nlieu de non-quasi-lissité. C’est un fermé de Zariski de X dont toutes les composantes\nirréductibles sont de dimension < d. Par hypothèse de récurrence, |f |(Y ) est un\nc-polytope de (R∗+ )n de dimension ⩽ d − 1. Son image réciproque |f |−1 (|f |(Y )) est\nun domaine analytique Γ-strict de X contenant Y . Comme Y \b→ X est sans bord, Y\nest contenu dans l’intérieur topologique de |f |−1 (|f |(Y )). Ce dernier apparaı̂t ainsi\ncomme un voisinage de Y dont l’image par |f | est un c-polytope de dimension\n⩽ d − 1.\n     Soit x ∈ X − Y . Il existe R ∈ Γ · |k ∗ | tel que X soit contenu\ndans le domaine aﬃnoı̈de W de X an déﬁni par les conditions |ai | ⩽ R. En vertu\ndu théorème de Gerritzen-Grauert (0.20.7), le domaine X est réunion ﬁnie de\ndomaines Γ-rationnels de W ; il suﬃt dès lors de traiter le cas où X est lui-même\nun domaine Γ-rationnel de W .\n     Il est alors déﬁni par une conjonction d’inégalités de la forme |gi | ⩽ λi |h| où les λi\nappartiennent à Γ et où (g1 , . . . , gr , h) engendre l’idéal des fonctions analytiques\nsur W . Cette dernière condition implique que h ne s’annule pas sur X (tout zéro\nde h sur X est un zéro commun à h et aux gi ), et l’on peut donc, par compacité,\n                                            1250007-40\n\f                            Espaces de Berkovich, polytopes, squelettes et théorie des modèles\n\n\nrajouter une condition de la forme |h| ⩾ r à la déﬁnition de X, où r est un élément\nconvenable de (Γ · |k ∗ |)Q . Il est dès lors immédiat que V ne change pas si l’on\nperturbe un peu les gi et h ; on peut par conséquent supposer que toutes ces\nfonctions appartiennent à A.\n    L’anneau des fonctions de la forme a/h\u0005 , où a ∈ A, et \u0010 ⩾ 0, est dense dans\nl’anneau des fonctions de X.
